# DawnTrader Batch Catalog

> **Purpose**: Master index of batches deployed to the DawnTrader system.
> **Updated by**: Langston (compiled from current repo docs, snapshot log, scope files, and available completion reports)
> **Location**: `1-system-manual/BATCH_CATALOG.md`
>
> **Note on commit hashes**: This catalog includes commit hashes where they could be verified from the frozen snapshot log, current CCPI, or other canonical governance material. Where a hash could not be recovered confidently from the currently accessible source set, the entry is marked `—` rather than guessing.

---

| B-COST-MATH-CONSOLIDATION (CC-C; Kyle-directed) | 2026-07-29 | `b0a7a7873` | **architecture. CI 4-green (`30503313569`), deployed restart#541, HTTP 200, 0 unstable restarts.** Kyle: *"scope them fully including looking into the history and historical intent."* **★ FIRST BATCH UNDER THE NEW §2 1.b PROVENANCE RULE, and the read CHANGED THE BATCH'S SHAPE TWICE.** Found `bridge/canonical/` §2 **internally inconsistent — F1+F2+F3 DOUBLE-COUNT SLIPPAGE** (F2 puts slippage inside the actual prices, F1 computes gross from them, F3 subtracts it again) ⇒ the design **cannot be satisfied as written**, which is *why* the code drifted. ⚠️ **Langston correction: Kyle's directive is CORROBORATED by F1, NOT authorised by it — two self-consistent resolutions existed; a self-contradictory document cannot be a tiebreaker.** Shipped: one pure `core/math/trade-pnl.ts` + `computeOpenPnl` as a **separate entry point on purpose** (open leg is a MARK and a MODELLED fee). **Bit-identity PROVEN with `toBe`, not `toBeCloseTo`**, against verbatim transcriptions of all three retired copies, written BEFORE the re-point. **★★ THE CENSUS WAS SCOPED BY PATTERN, NOT QUANTITY** — re-scoped to *"computes **or ASSERTS** a relationship among MONETARY quantities"* it went **3 → 9 real sites + 1 benign**. Langston's two fences (`as any` on a schema-typed row; `.tsc-baseline.json` as a registry of MUTED phantom reads) each found sites mine could not — **and NEITHER closes the census alone** (a site with no cast is invisible to the first). **★ FOUR BROKEN SELF-CHECKS:** one always-RED (**339 false alarms since 07-15** — read `portfolio_state.startingBalance`, a column that DOES NOT EXIST, and fell back to the displayed balance ⇒ degenerated to *"is realized P&L zero?"*), one always-GREEN (fell back to `0` ⇒ **structurally unreachable**; Langston's falsifiable prediction of 0 warnings **tested at 0 on both streams**), and **one I broke while fixing it** — re-anchoring made both sides the same number, converting always-RED to always-GREEN, **the exact failure this batch flagged, on the worse polarity**; demoted to OBSERVED, real invariant homed **#620**. **VERIFIED LIVE: zero MISMATCH in 32 min post-deploy while 60 OBSERVED fired** ⇒ path executes, alarms gone. **★★ AND IT ENDED AT THE RISK ENVELOPE:** site 9 (`guardrail-settings.getPortfolioBalanceV2`) feeds the **DAILY-LOSS KILL-SWITCH denominator** (`daily-loss-budget.ts:135`, CC-C-verified) + `SizingContext` both classes, with the same frozen anchor + capped session-scoped sum ⇒ **it divides by a figure TOO LARGE so loss% reads TOO SMALL and trips LATER than the approved limit. Langston VOIDED HIS OWN P19-B6 approval**, which had rested on the denominator shrinking. ⚠️ **MAGNITUDE UNMEASURED — direction certain only; paper mode, no capital exposed.** Restated as **#618** (retitled to name the MECHANISM, consumers table, risk paths marked). Also closed a **governance gap: `c5-financial-diagnostics.ts` had NO SIM entry at all** despite 3 checks on the live money path — now **SIM 9.15**, with the absence recorded as the likely reason the checks survived. Behaviour change NAMED not smuggled: site 8's fabricated `'0'` → explicit **409** (⚠️ and the fabrication **survives one hop client-side**, incl. a made-up `status:'OK'` — filed, absence claim **WITHDRAWN** for want of a census). tsc delta **0 / −1**, the baseline gate NAMING the removed error; 21/21 tests. | `Scope Files/B_COST_MATH_CONSOLIDATION_SCOPE.md` + `..._PRE_AUDIT.md` | `Batch Completion/B_COST_MATH_CONSOLIDATION_COMPLETION_REPORT.md` |
| B-COST-ACCOUNTING-HONESTY (CC-C; Kyle-directed) | 2026-07-28 | `c4b9abfb5` | **non_architecture. CI 4-green (30356720024), deployed restart#537, HTTP 200.** Reported as "negative slippage flips losses green (ONDO stop-loss +6.98%)" — **premise FALSE and reported as such BEFORE coding (rule-24 outcome 2/3):** net_pnl equalled true economics on **293/293** closed trades with fills incl. all 57 negative-cost rows, 0 divergences; the ONDO gain is real. Old gross was measured vs INTENDED prices while cost DEDUCTED slippage — the two distortions **cancel exactly**, so the obvious fix (clamp cost ≥ 0) would have **BROKEN a correct net on 57 rows**. Kyle chose gross-off-actual-fills. **Fix:** `gross = (actualExit − actualEntry) × qty`; `totalCost = fees only`; slippage retained as signed telemetry (reported, not deducted); % denominator → actual entry value (**§9.2: ONDO +6.98% → +7.66%**, cutover 11:57Z). **★ §9.5 census: the formula lived at THREE sites** (engine close, manual close, open display) — the scope's own first draft said one; fixing only the engine would have made hand-closed and engine-closed trades disagree. Langston additionally found **asymmetric persistence** at the manual-close site (latent, 0 live instances — all 71 non-proof rows are `never_filled`, no fill BY CONSTRUCTION); fixed at the find. Industry-grounded (Perold IS / Harris Ch.21 / Zipline / GIPS / PRIIPs / SEC 605). Net **provably unchanged: 298/298 live**, tsc delta 0, 26 tests incl. a 3-site fence that caught my own carve-out reasoning being wrong. §9.3 homed to task `verify-cost-accounting-honesty`. | `Scope Files/B_COST_ACCOUNTING_HONESTY_SCOPE.md` | `Batch Completion/B_COST_ACCOUNTING_HONESTY_COMPLETION_REPORT.md` |
| B-PROMOTION-RACE-FIX (#508; CC-C) | 2026-07-28 | `46cba2b9c` | **architecture. CI 4-green (30316335026), deployed restart#535, verified; #508 RESOLVED.** Fixed the defect behind 3 orphaned `closed_trades` records (MET/ETH/AVAX): rows with entry data, no live position, never closeable. **TWO independent defects** — (1) `checkRtbPromotion` had NO single-flight latch across its three triggers (TCL_ACTIVATED / TRADE_CLOSED / setInterval), so two concurrent passes promoted+opened the SAME signal; (2) `createActiveOpenPosition` CAUGHT the `unique(symbol)` 23505 and silently returned the winner's row, so the loser continued as if opened, stranding its record. Plus (3) the in-pass half (pre-loop `openPositions` snapshot never refreshed). **Fix:** coalescing single-flight latch (`isRunning`-guarded per Langston's blocker — the re-run was the only trigger without stop-safety; a deploy restart is exactly that interleaving), discriminated `{position, created}` return + caller deletes its own orphan, `promotedSymbolsThisPass` in-pass guard, `deleteClosedTrade` structurally restricted to `closedAt IS NULL`. tsc delta ZERO (measured); 18 tests incl. a **mutation-proved** source fence; 3 legacy rows deleted Kyle-authorized → final census **zero orphans**, balance verified unchanged. Rule-24.a note: three earlier causes were proposed and each killed by data (Kyle's "when were they opened?" / "why only 3?"). | `Scope Files/B_PROMOTION_RACE_FIX_SCOPE.md` | `Batch Completion/B_PROMOTION_RACE_FIX_COMPLETION_REPORT.md` |

Tier sequencing: Supabase Micro → Small (Kyle upgrade during dedup) → Medium ($60/mo, needed for SPY chunked path) → Small (back to $15/mo post-ship — write IO dropped ~20× from dedup, read IO ~5×). | **Closes the structural root-cause of the 18-56× row duplication in B74 archive that B-NEW-34 surfaced (DISTINCT ON CTE workaround at aggregator level) and B-NEW-34b's snapshot architecture could not bridge alone.** Kyle directive 2026-05-19 ("we shouldn't be satisfied with these blue chip xStocks not populating") re-sequenced B-NEW-35 ahead of B-NEW-36 after B-NEW-34b's 26 heavy-symbol pre-warm statement_timeouts persisted across `--days 14`/`--days 7`/`--days 3`. **Three layers of dedup protection:** (a) PostgreSQL UNIQUE constraint on `(symbol, interval_begin)` for all three partitioned `_ohlc_1m` tables — DB physically rejects duplicates; (b) Drizzle `.onConflictDoUpdate()` clause in `server/services/passive-archive/ohlc-batch-writer.ts` — archiver UPSERTs evolving fields per Kraken WS contract (latest tick IS the correct cumulative OHLCV per minute); (c) in-buffer `Map<string, Insert...>` dedup BEFORE the chunked INSERT — required because Kraken sends multiple updates per minute that all land in the same 5s buffer; a single INSERT cannot target the same conflict-key twice per PG semantics; Map insertion-order gives "last wins" naturally. **Cleanup volume:** ~23.2M duplicate rows removed across three tables (~84% reduction). xstock_perp_2026_05: 3.22M rows deleted (97% reduction). xstock_spot_2026_05: 14M+ deleted (main pass + retry + SPY per-day chunked path). crypto_spot_2026_05: 6.4M+ deleted. (2) ON CONFLICT DO UPDATE rejects single-INSERT-with-duplicate-conflict-target via "cannot affect row a second time"; archivers that buffer multi-tick per minute need in-buffer dedup BEFORE the UPSERT call. (3) ADD UNIQUE on actively-written tables requires `pm2 stop` window or fresh duplicates landed in the lock-acquisition window will fail the constraint. **Five-symbol snapshot gap finding (handoff to B-NEW-36 sub-batch (c)):** xstock_spot_ohlc_60m_snapshot has 260 symbols, not 265. | **Batch B-NEW-40 (pg pool keepalive + TEC refresh timeout — silent-TCP-death root-cause fix)** | 2026-05-17 | ✅ CLOSED (Kyle ack 2026-05-17). Commits `6a70b45c4` + `62890eaf0` + `5f156c297`. Deploy 2026-05-17T12:46:47Z (PM2 #290). 14-day soak armed (alert b83b1e4b, triggers 2026-05-31T12:46:47Z). Files: `server/db.ts` (pool config), `server/services/trailing-exit-controller.ts` (45s `Promise.race` timeout fence + diagnostic accessor), `server/routes.ts` (`/api/diagnostics/tec-config` route + Central Clock health enrichment), `server/tests/unit/b-new-40-tec-refresh-hang.test.ts` (NEW), `/usr/local/bin/tec-pg-capture` on staging (adds `ss -tnpi` capture per snapshot tick). PM2 restart required to deploy `server/db.ts` pool changes. CI checks pending push. | **Closes two stacked root contributors to the recurring `TEC_STALE_FAIL_CLOSED` cascade (2026-05-15 17:13 UTC and 2026-05-16 11:14 UTC, both required PM2 restarts).** Cause 1 (network layer): silent TCP path death between Hetzner Falkenstein and Supabase Frankfurt; the default pg-pool `keepAlive: false` lets dead-but-ESTABLISHED sockets stay in the pool indefinitely; reused for a query, the query hangs forever (no socket error, no statement timeout). Cause 2 (code amplifier introduced by B79.TEC 2026-05-08): the per-asset-class fire-and-forget refresh pattern with `tecConfigRefreshInFlight` Map coalescer + 5-min staleness ceiling converts a single hung promise into a permanent fail-closed (`.catch`/`.finally` never fire because the underlying promise neither resolves nor rejects). Pre-audit confirmed via strict-regex grep: 0 `[TEC_REFRESH_FAIL]` events across 4832 `[TEC_STALE_FAIL_CLOSED]` events. Pre-May 8 heartbeat-cycle slowdowns existed throughout April (14.9s and 96.9s outliers observed) but were absorbed quietly by the old await-based TEC architecture — the network condition was always there, B79.TEC turned it catastrophic. **Five mitigation layers in one batch (cause + code amplifier + observability + regression coverage + governance):** (1) `server/db.ts` pool config — `keepAlive: true` + `keepAliveInitialDelayMillis: 10_000` (OS TCP probes detect dead sockets in ~12min instead of 2+ hours; in-flight queries on detected-dead clients reject cleanly with no silent retry, aligns with CLAUDE.md §5 #15 NO PATCHES doctrine "no silent fallbacks"), `query_timeout: 30_000` (pg-client-side abort on any single query >30s — safety net for any other hang source), `idleTimeoutMillis: 30_000` (extends idle window from default 10s to 30s; lower connection churn since keepalive carries resilience), explicit `max: 10` (matches default, surfaces ceiling for operator visibility), `application_name: 'dawntrader_main'` (tags this connection class in `pg_stat_activity` and Supabase dashboard for DB-side diagnosability). Boot emits `[DB_POOL_INIT]` log line confirming config landed. (2) `trailing-exit-controller.ts` refresh-promise timeout fence — wraps `refreshTECConfigForClass(assetClass)` in `Promise.race([refresh, timeoutAfter45s])`; on timeout, existing `.catch` increments `tecRefreshFailCount` and logs `[TEC_REFRESH_TIMEOUT]` (distinct tag from `[TEC_REFRESH_FAIL]` for path attribution), `.finally` clears the in-flight Map. 45s budget = pool `query_timeout` (30s) + 15s slack. Result: F and J ran K's pure-SL/TP semantic, producing identical results. **Net effect on prior calibration data:** the "remove BE-stop adds 0.090 P&L" finding actually measures K (remove BOTH BE + trailing). We had no isolated measurement of "BE alone" vs "trailing alone" effects. **Fix:** two new dedicated simulators. **`replayNoBeWithTrailingTake` (F)** walks bars in pre-target phase checking only original SL + target hit (no BE-lock at +1×ATR); on target hit, switches to trailing-after-target (moonbag) phase and exits at trailMultiplier × ATR retracement from peak. **`replayBeOnlyNoTrail` (J)** walks bars with BE-stop logic enabled (latch BE at +1×ATR trigger; thereafter SL = entry, TP = target); on target hit exits at target — NO trailing afterwards. K's `replayPureSlTp` unchanged (correct for K's intended behavior). **Once next 04:00 UTC replay-ablation cron runs**, F and J will produce differentiated results. Within 7-10 days we'll have clean data to distinguish BE-stop alone vs trailing alone vs both. K's +0.090 vs A signal stands as "remove all post-entry protection beats baseline at Sharpe 1.84." | **Batch 69.2 (b67_2 phase preference visibility fix)** | 2026-05-04 | `1efb1599` (2 files: vts-runner.ts + signal-orchestrator.ts +20/-2). PM2 #139. | **Calibration aggregator visibility fix.** Triggered by Kyle 2026-05-04 review of Factor Calibration table showing `b67_2_phase_preference` at 100% shift=0 across all trades. Investigation found the factor IS firing correctly on every trade — the calibration aggregator's `shift = real_decision.confidence - alternate_decision.confidence` was structurally zero because both fields received the same value: `real_decision.confidence` = `predictiveConfidence ?? 0.5` (set by `emitAblationRecord` caller, shared across all factor rows for a trade), and `alternate_decision.confidence` = `_baseConf` for b67_2 (which equals `predictiveConfidence`). Same value → shift = 0 by construction. The actual modulated value lived in `metadata.confidence_with_phase_pref` where the aggregator never read it. **Fix:** change b67_2 emit hook in both vts-runner + signal-orchestrator to set `alternateDecision.confidence = modulated` (with-factor value). Then `shift = predictiveConfidence × (1 - weight)` correctly captures the b67_2 effect direction. Convention deviation flagged in code comments — other multiplicative factors store without-factor in alt.confidence, but b67_2 uses with-factor because it's the FIRST factor in the chain (its without-factor value coincides with `real_decision.confidence` by construction). **Deeper finding logged:** the framework's "shift" metric isn't actually measuring per-factor effect for ANY modulator — `real_decision.confidence` is the raw classifier value, not the chain-final. Multiplicative factors LOOK like they're working because compounding makes their shifts non-zero, but magnitude isn't a clean per-factor measurement. Predictive-lift column is the trustworthy decision-grade metric. Proper framework fix queued for a later cleanup batch. | (no separate scope file — bug fix per Kyle directive 2026-05-04) | B69 closure report extended with B69.2 section. |

Pure-function score + narrow-band confidence factor + ablation row architecture mirrors B67.4 / B68.4 exactly. **Score formula** (Langston cc-inbox #880 §B.1 pick): `score = SUM(volume[i] × sign(close[i] - close[i-1])) / SUM(volume[i])` over rolling N=30 bars; bounded [-1, +1]. **Factor**: `clamp(0.92, 1.05, 1.0 + score × 0.05)`. Symmetric ±0.40 ACCUMULATION/DISTRIBUTION metadata thresholds. **§D.1 (Langston cc-inbox #880):** `has_liquidation_spike` metadata flag (any single bar in lookback with volume > 5× lookback median; pure pass-through compute on same N-bar slice; segments calibration cohorts clean vs liquidation-contaminated without recomputation). **§D.2 (Langston cc-inbox #881):** liquidation-spike multiplier shipped as 8th `module_constant` from v1 (not v1-hardcoded). **MCE 7-group orchestrator** (was 6): added `refreshVolumeRegimeConfig()` resolving 8 keys; both first-refresh Promise.all and subsequent-refresh per-group try/catch arrays extended; B67.4 hotfix-#2 try/catch wrapper inherited unchanged; `assembleRegimeConfig` unchanged (volume regime is chain-only, not part of `RegimeConfig`). 3 new public accessors. **Modulation chain after this batch:** `raw × macro × phase × freshness × outcome × volume_regime → clamp [0.4, 1.0]`. vts-runner emit hook updates `openTrade.regimeConfidenceModulated` so closed VTS trades carry full 5-modulator chain composite. Active-path orchestrator computes chain for B68.2 ablation metadata only — same any-cast on `MarketContext.ohlcData` deferred to B67.5 per Langston cc-inbox #881 D.1 (RUNNING_ISSUES #44 carries this with B68.5). **8 module_constants** in new `volume_regime` module: lookback_bars (30), accumulation_threshold (0.40), distribution_threshold (-0.40), factor_min (0.92), factor_max (1.05), sensitivity (0.05), min_samples (30), liquidation_spike_multiplier (5.0). **3 new test files** (16 cases total): cold-start, pure accumulation/distribution, alternating, zero-volume edge, clamp boundaries, liquidation-spike detection (flag at 6× / no-flag at 4×), label thresholds, lookback respects N, counterfactual divide-out. **Verified post-deploy PM2 #128:** all 8 expected factor types emitting in regime_factor_alternates (b67_1×3 + b67_2_phase_preference + b67_4_outcome_feedback + b68_4_regime_age + b68_5_path_b_sustainability + **b68_2_volume_regime NEW**). Acceptable for variant-comparability per Langston. (b) Extended OHLC window from `exitTime + 1h` to `entryTime + maxHoldMs` (7d) regardless of actual exit. Three-layer protection: (1) PostgreSQL UNIQUE constraint on `(symbol, interval_begin)` for all three partitioned `_ohlc_1m` tables, (2) Drizzle `.onConflictDoUpdate()` clause at `ohlc-batch-writer.ts:147-164` replacing prior plain INSERT, (3) in-buffer Map dedup at `:105-114` with insertion-order last-wins (required because Kraken sends multiple WS updates per minute that all land in the same 5s buffer; PostgreSQL throws "ON CONFLICT cannot affect row a second time" when a single INSERT targets the same conflict-key twice). Cleanup volume ~23.2M duplicate rows removed across three tables (~84% reduction). Phase 1 cleanup shipped via bash-per-symbol pattern after 5 SQL revisions hit Postgres `statement_timeout` (PL/pgSQL DO-block LOOP wallclock accumulates against the cap regardless of internal COMMITs — institutional-memory rule now codified in SIM). Each false alert then routed to Langston to triage and jammed his single worker (the "Langston stuck" Kyle saw was this flood feeding back). Fix: all commit + file reads now route through `GOV_REF` (origin ref) via `git ls-tree`/`git show` after a fetch — it grades the actual pushed state regardless of the working tree (Langston Step-4 APPROVED, ~1-min turnaround; flagged 2 non-blocking fast-follows). The 37 false alerts were resolved + the checker muted-then-fixed-then-relived in one session. §13 follow-up #347 (pin GOV_CUTOFF to a committed value so it can't be accidentally reset — Langston). Learning: the flood was a ONE-TIME backlog draining (built up while the checker was off), NOT a steady-state failure — grandfathering + forward-grading is the steady state. | (Kyle directive; prior B-GOV/B-GOV-2 reports) | (this catalog row + #347) |

Langston Step-2 ruling (4 questions) + Step-4 APPROVED as-is after reviewing the DEPLOYED sources.** ROOT CAUSE, three verified facts: `_send_chunks` splits at 2000 CHARS posting each chunk as its own Discord message; `first_id` is captured in-loop and returned once so it is **structurally sender-log-only and never reaches the wire** (this killed the originally-proposed group-by-`first_id` fix at pre-audit, before any code); the anchored `ADDRESS_START_RE` gate runs **pre-enqueue**, so chunks 2..N were discarded before becoming tasks — silent, no error either side. **★ ADJACENT DEFECT found in pre-audit and fixed same batch (Langston-ruled): the `--notify` mention was prepended as `<@id> `, and `<` is not in the gate's allowed leading class ⇒ a notify-flagged dispatch to Langston was dropped ENTIRELY, not truncated.** SHIPPED: group marker on every chunk of a multi-chunk **Langston-addressed** dispatch (so Kyle-facing + §10.5 alert traffic stay byte-identical BY CONSTRUCTION); mention applied AFTER the address token; reassembly buffered by group id **above** the gate (Langston's load-bearing ordering requirement); incomplete groups flush with an explicit `[INCOMPLETE CHUNK GROUP]` note, never a silent hold. **Both Langston findings fixed IN-BATCH, not deferred (rule 23):** (A) `t0` refreshed per received part so the timeout measures SILENCE not group AGE — closes a fragment-into-two-invokes window under 429 backoff; (B) `split_on_whitespace` so a `file:line`/sha/path can never be cut mid-token — **CC-A and CC-B escalated (B) independently and were right; I had wrongly filed it cosmetic.** Full byte-fidelity considered and REJECTED: it needs slices beginning/ending with whitespace and Discord normalises that on send, so it would have been silently wrong. VERIFIED: 4 send-side unit cases; token integrity across 60 boundary offsets ALL PASS; 4 live reassemblies incl. a 4,165-char seam-straddling test with `path:line` planted AT the seam (intact, exact) and **two genuine unplanned production cases (CC-B 2,219 / CC-A 2,134 chars, zero change on their side)**; Langston quoted back a proof token deliberately placed in the FINAL chunk. **★ PROCESS FINDING recorded as debt not precedent:** deploy preceded Step-4 — an exception earned by change-class + active-corruption of the review channel + ~10s reversibility, **NOT** a precedent that `non_architecture` skips the gate (Langston's words). **★ TWO OWNED MISSES:** (1) the pre-audit asserted "Helsinki infra, NOT the repo" — WRONG; the fix lived only on the deployed copy and the next `deploy.sh` would have silently reverted it (ported, byte-identical, `c000e31b8`); (2) NO SCOPE FILE was written (design-input doc treated as the scope), so the `change-class:` had nothing to parse and the checker correctly defaulted to `architecture` — 4 alerts raised, retroactive scope written. #553 RESOLVED. | `B_COMMS_CHUNK_FIX_SCOPE.md` (retroactive) + `B_COMMS_CHUNK_FIX_PRE_AUDIT.md` + `Change Lists/B_COMMS_CHUNK_FIX_CHANGE_LIST.md` | `B_COMMS_CHUNK_FIX_COMPLETION_REPORT.md` |

### B-XSTOCK-FEED-LIVENESS (#594) — xStock feed silent-stall watchdog reads a DATA clock (2026-07-31, CC-B)

**change-class:** `non_architecture` · **Code:** `0464c8219` → `e93136f81` · **Owner:** CC-B
**What was broken:** the xStock feed's silent-stall watchdog thresholded on a field stamped by ANY frame — heartbeats and subscribe-acks included. ⇒ **a feed delivering chatter and ZERO prices read as perfectly healthy**, on the only venue price source for tokenized equities, and the watchdog is a named prerequisite for xStock active-fill enablement.
**Provenance (§2 1.b / rule 24.0) — it changed the fix:** at `ce4a7e408` (B74) the field had exactly two consumers, the stamp and a health-log line, and that commit contains **zero** `runStallWatchdogTick`. ⇒ original intent was CONNECTION liveness, for which stamping on any frame is CORRECT. **The defect is the second attachment (P19-B4a C3), not the field.** ⇒ **rule 24 outcome (3): ADD a clock, never repoint the existing one** — repointing would have silently inverted the health log with nothing throwing and no test failing.
**Shipped:** `lastDataMsgAt` stamped only in the parsers (after each guard), seeded at ws-open; watchdog re-pointed to it through the unchanged RTH/off-RTH + weekend selection; `last_data_msg_age_ms` added to the 60s health line (without it the new clock is invisible outside the process and #635/#636 are unobservable).
★ **The lesson worth carrying:** the first six fences proved the READER while the defect was a WRITER — **restoring the exact bug left all six GREEN.** Langston caught it at Step-8; six stamp-site fences now drive real frames through `handleMessage`, **mutation-proved (bug restored ⇒ 4 FAIL, exactly the four no-price frames)**. 19/19 restored.
⚠️ **A void measurement, recorded:** `[STALL]` = zero was grepped from `out.log`; `[STALL]` is `console.error` ⇒ `error.log`. **That zero was void either way.** Re-derived on the right stream with a positive control: **0 across ~13 days RETAINED — "retained," not "ever."**
**Not fixed here, homed:** **#635** (universe-wide clock vs per-symbol-derived thresholds ⇒ TOTAL-stall detection only; partial-stall blindness) · **#636** (snap-arrival ≠ mark-freshness). **Siblings** stamp identically but have no watchdog ⇒ no defect today; any watchdog added later inherits the trap.
**Governance:** SIM content updated. **System Manual N/A** (judged explicitly — feed-liveness instrumentation, no architecture/strategy/regime/filter/pipeline/math change).

### B-GOV-HEARTBEAT-REPAIR (#637) — the governance checker's dead-man switch could not clear its own alarm (2026-08-01, CC-B)

**change-class:** `non_architecture` · **Code:** `49a41aee4` → `28b029528` · CI 4/4 job-by-job · Langston Step-4 APPROVED at the ref.
**What was broken:** the heartbeat resolved without `--evidence`, mandatory since B-GOV-INTEGRITY-1; every call exited non-zero, the `catch` swallowed it, and the alert id was discarded **in the same statement** ⇒ the alert could never clear and nothing retained the handle. **Not latent — it fails on first use; it had simply never been used, because the timer had been off for ~6 weeks** (measured off the heartbeat state file's mtime, with the poller's own state file as the positive control).
**Why it matters:** the checker's healthy output is SILENCE, so a dead checker is indistinguishable from a clean repo without this switch.
**Shipped:** shared `resolveEvidenceOrSentinel()` SSOT; poller publishes `gradedRefSha` across the process boundary (NULL on the fetch-fail path, which returns before the sha is set); id nulled only on confirmed clear; benign-failure test anchored to the CLI's actual message.
★ **Two catches worth carrying:** (1) **the fence caught a fabricated-provenance bug in the fix** — an all-hex timestamp would have been persisted as a git sha, and the server's validator is unanchored so it would NOT have been caught downstream; (2) **`systemctl enable` produced `enabled`+`active` with NO next firing** — monotonic triggers with no anchor — so the batch nearly closed on a green that meant nothing.
**Verified provoked end-to-end** (a clean run exercises neither branch). **System Manual N/A**, judged explicitly.

### B-DEPLOY-LOCK (#649, closes #140) — the sanctioned, locked staging deploy path (2026-08-06, CC-B)

**change-class:** `non_architecture` · final `280768887` · CI 4/4 job-verified on both deployed shas · Langston Steps 1/2/4/8 ALL APPROVED (4+2+1+1 revision rounds, 11 substantive catches).
**What:** `dt-deploy <full-sha>` replaces the hand-typed deploy chain that let three concurrent sessions silently overwrite each other mid-verification (#647 class: the loser is not told they lost). Locks atomically outside the repo; refuses concurrency (holder+sha+since + the #540 tier-3 protocol in the refusal), dirt (fail-loud, protected 10 real items), and unreviewed refs; conditional `npm ci`; **migrate in-chain — the SYSTEM_MANUAL:12734 invariant restored, closing #140 (open since 05-25)**; post-conditions asserted at the objects (live HEAD, build-identity response, ENGINE RESUMED) with the record written only after they pass, including durable migrate evidence. **Proven by two real self-deploys (windows 15s/11s vs the watchdog's 300s bound) plus three provoked refusals.** Six prescriptive doc sites + two Step-8 survivors swept (wide-grep clean); the dormant EC2-era CI deployer DELETED per rule 18.
**The batch's lesson:** every gate caught something real the prior gate missed — including a poll loop that died on iteration one of every real deploy while all refusal tests passed, and a sweep-completeness claim built on a narrow grep. **Enforcement = the lock + the docs offering nothing to copy; detection = the record + the three-branch restart discriminator.**

### B-HOTFIX-DEPLOY-BY (#656) — deploy records name their session (2026-08-06, CC-B)

**change-class:** `hotfix` · final `d0e4a65f1` · CI 4/4 · Langston CHANGES-NEEDED → APPROVED (1 round, 4 real catches).
**What:** `dt-deploy` now REQUIRES `--by <session>` (refuse-not-guess; charset-validated; `-*` values and duplicate flags refuse — the reviewer proved an unset variable in the documented full form could otherwise silently skip `--pre-restart`). Record: `deployed_by` → `deployed_by_claimed` + `deployed_via` (claim labelled a claim; zero readers of the old field, record truncated per-run). Lock holder names the session, so concurrency refusals attribute. 7 prescriptive sites swept; FIX-2026-08-06-A; SIM record fields; observation procedure updated; playbook rules 15+16 added (Kyle-directed same pass). Installed from blob, sha256-verified, refusals provoked live.


### B-COMMS-IMAGES (#657) — two-way Discord image support (2026-08-06/07, Infra Claude)

**change-class:** `non_architecture` · commits `8b6b733ab` + `875664329` (Step-4 fix) · Langston Step-4 CHANGES-NEEDED -> fast-pass PROCEED (1 blocker: the `image_atts` reader shipped with NO WRITER — the capability was dead-from-birth and every restart would have passed; his hash-verified base-lineage check + writer/reader parity census caught it) · deployed 2026-08-06 22:02Z via atomic wait-then-deploy (backups `.pre-images-20260806-220224`, 4/4 sha256 repo<->server identical).
**What:** both bridges save inbound image attachments off-loop to `/var/log/cc-discord-media/<date>/` (sanitized filenames; `media_paths`/`media_failed` on the inbox entry; a failed save is stated as FAILED, never an empty set); Langston's prompt lists THIS message's paths for native Read; `cc-send --file` uploads (telegram backend refuses, never drops); Langston `[[ATTACH]]` outbound with startup-realpath'd allowlist (`outbox/`, `exports/`), markers stripped always, refusals posted loudly; daily counted prune >60d (transit-buffer doctrine). **Step-8 LIVE 3/3 PASS:** inbound red 64x64 read from the PNG header; blue outbox upload mirrored (`langston_outbound_media`); `/etc/passwd` probe refused in-channel ("outside allowlist") with the file never posted.
**The batch's lesson:** the census format caught its own author — the pre-audit ASSERTED the enqueue writer while the diff did not contain it; a census that asserts a writer which doesn't exist is precisely the failure it exists to catch, and only the reviewer's parity check stood between that sentence and a silently-dead capability in production.
